1
introduction
Uniform rectifiability is the quantitative counterpart of classical rectifiability. While rectifiabil-
ity asserts that a set can be covered, up to a negligible part, by countably many Lipschitz images,
uniform rectifiability requires this parametrization to occur in a stable, scale-invariant way. Since
the foundational work of David and Semmes, uniformly rectifiable sets have been understood
as precisely those Ahlfors regular sets on which the basic tools of Calderón–Zygmund theory
retain their Euclidean behavior.
Among the weakest and most natural of these conditions is the weak approximation of
Lipschitz functions by affine functions, usually abbreviated WALA. Informally, WALA says that
every Lipschitz function on the set admits good affine approximations at most locations and
scales, with the exceptional scales satisfying a Carleson packing estimate. Thus WALA may be
viewed as a quantitative Rademacher theorem on rough sets: instead of asking for differentiability
almost everywhere, one asks that failure of affine approximation be quantitatively sparse across
space and scale.
David and Semmes proved in [4] that uniformly rectifiable sets satisfy WALA. They also
proved the converse in dimension 1 and the converse in higher dimensions has remained open.
The difficulty is conceptual as well as technical. The conjecture asks whether the analytic
differentiability information encoded by Lipschitz functions is strong enough to recover the
large-scale geometric organization of the underlying measure. In this paper we answer this
question affirmatively. Our main theorem is the following.
Theorem 1.1 (WALA conjecture). Suppose µ is an α-AD-regular measure on Rn satisfying the WALA
condition, see Definition 8.1 for the formal definition. Then α ∈N and µ is uniformly rectifiable.
We briefly describe the strategy of the proof. First, we prove a qualitative type theorem. We
show that if on a AD-regular set the distance of Lipschitz functions from affine functions almost
everywhere goes to 0 at small scales, then the measure is rectifiable. This gives the α ∈N.
Secondly, we introduce a new, quantitative version of Alberti decomposition and prove that
AD-regular sets can be written as layers of invariant cubes along certain directions and having
very small width along the orthogonal ones. This allows us to perform a construction similar in
spirit to the classical techniques à la Alberti-Csörnyei-Preiss, but in a quantitative way.
What we prove in the end is even more, we are able to prove the so called GWALA conjecture,
where one substitutes affine functions with any finite dimensional vector space of Borel functions.
In a future version of the paper this is how the main theorem will be stated.

preliminaries
3
This is a preliminary version of the paper. Some applications and complementary results are
not yet included in the present draft. In forthcoming sections, written jointly with Michele Villa
and Mihalis Mourgoglou, we will develop two further directions.
Together with Michele Villa, we will prove the converse of our qualitative result Theorem 7.6,
providing a full characterization of rectifiability with approximability of Lipschitz functions by
affine fuctions. Secondly, we will provide a proof of the fact that the Many Segment Property,
see [2] implies uniform rectifiability exploiting these techniques.
Together with Mihalis Mourgoglou, we study the following free boundary problems associated
with the Regularity problem. Let L be a uniformly elliptic divergence-form operator with L∞
coefficients, and let Ω⊂Rn+1 be an open set.
• If ∂Ωis n-Ahlfors regular and the Regularity problem (RL
1) is solvable, then ∂Ωis uniformly
n-rectifiable.
• Let 0 < Hn(∂Ω) < ∞, and assume that Ωis Wiener regular and satisfies a mild connectivity
condition near boundary points. Suppose moreover that for every f ∈Lipc(∂Ω), the
solution u f to the continuous Dirichlet problem for L with boundary data f satisfies
N (∇u f )(ξ) < ∞for Hn|∂Ω-almost every ξ ∈∂Ω, where N stands for the modified
non-tangential maximal function. Then ∂Ωis n-rectifiable.
Concerning future directions, the author has a work in progress studying the above problem
in charted metric spaces, where the targets of charts are Carnot groups, since the techniques
introduced in Rn in this paper extend without many difficulties.
Acknowledgments. A.M. was also partially supported by Ikerbasque and by the European
Union’s Horizon Europe research and innovation programme under the Marie Skłodowska-Curie
grant agreement no 101065346.

Since dR is Borel with respect to the vague topology, the right-hand side is a countable infimum
of Borel functions. Thus x 7→∆R(x, r) is Borel, after the chosen arbitrary extension on the

We now prove the convergence. Let G be the full µ-measure set of points x for which the
normalized blow-ups µ(B(x, ρ))−1Tx,ρµ, 0 < ρ < ρ0, are precompact in the vague topology for
every ρ0 > 0. This follows from Lemma 3.6 and the asymptotic doubling assumption.
Fix x ∈G. Suppose by contradiction that limρ→0 ∆R(x, ρ) ̸= 0. Since ∆R(x, ρ) ≥0, there
exist ε > 0 and a sequence ρi ↓0 such that ∆R(x, ρi) ≥ε for every i. By precompactness, after
passing to a subsequence, µ(B(x, ρi))−1Tx,ρiµ ⇀ν for some Radon measure ν. By the definition
of tangent measures, ν ∈Tan(µ, x).
Therefore ∆R(x, ρi) ≤dR(µ(B(x, ρi))−1Tx,ρiµ, ν) for every i. Since µ(B(x, ρi))−1Tx,ρiµ ⇀ν, the
right-hand side tends to 0. This contradicts ∆R(x, ρi) ≥ε. Hence limρ→0 ∆R(x, ρ) = 0 for every
x ∈G, and therefore for µ-almost every x.

Lemma 6.1. Let µ be a Radon measure. Then, there are n orthonormal Borel vector fields e1, . . . , en :
Rn →Rn such that for µ-almost every x ∈Rn we have that {e1, . . . , ed(x)} span V(µ, x), where
d(x) := dim(V(µ, x)).
Proof. This can be achieved by observing that the maps x 7→ΠV(µ,x) are Borel and by projecting
an orthonormal basis of Rn on V(µ, x).
Proposition 6.2. There exists a constant εn depending only on n such that if V ∈Gr(Rn) and
d(V, W) < εn then dim V = dim W.
Proof. For k = 0, . . . , n, set Grk(Rn) := {V ∈Gr(Rn) : dim V = k}. Each Grk(Rn) is com-
pact, and the sets Grk(Rn) are pairwise disjoint.

Proposition 10.1. Suppose R is an (e, σ, ε, δ, ζ, m, A)-invariant cube. Then, for every γ ∈G (R) and
every q ∈γ ∩2(A −ε)BR, we have
B(q, (δ + ε) diam R) ∩supp µ ̸= ∅.
Proof. Fix γ ∈G (R). By definition,
H 1(2ABR ∩γ \ B(supp µ, δ diam R)) ≤ε diam R.
Let us show that for every w ∈γ ∩2(A −ε)BR we have
dist(w, supp µ) ≤(δ + ε) diam R.
Indeed, if this was not the case, there would exist w0 ∈γ ∩2(A −ε)BR such that
B(w0, (δ + ε) diam R) ∩supp µ = ∅.
This implies in particular that
B(w0, ε diam R) ∩B(supp µ, δ diam R) = ∅.
Moreover, since w0 ∈2(A −ε)BR, we have B(w0, ε diam R) ⊆2ABR . Hence
H 1(2ABR ∩γ \ B(supp µ, δ diam R)) ≥H 1(γ ∩B(w0, ε diam R)).
Since γ is a C(e, σ)-curve and passes through the center of the ball B(w0, ε diam R), we have
H 1(γ ∩B(w0, ε diam R)) ≥2ε diam R.
Therefore
H 1(2ABR ∩γ \ B(supp µ, δ diam R)) ≥2ε diam R,
which is a contradiction.

This shows that there exists a constant c(C1, D, k, d) depending only on C1, D, k and on d such
that if ϑ ≤c(C1, D, k, d), then the set of cubes Ij(ϑ, σ, ε, δ, ζ, m, A) must have packing. This
concludes the proof.
Theorem 12.5. Suppose µ is a k-AD-regular measure with regularity constant D. Suppose µ satisfies
the WALA condition with constants {C(t) : t > 0}. Then µ is uniformly rectifiable.
Proof. Thanks to Theorem 12.4, we know that if parameters are chosen sufficiently small, then
the families of cubes Ij(ϑ, σ, ε, δ, ζ, m, A) have packing for every0 ≤j ≤k −1. Further, thanks to
Proposition 10.2 we infer that up to packing µ satisfies the OUWGL for every ε sufficiently small,
we refer to [4, Chapter II, Definition 3.13] for a definition. Then, [4, Chapter II, §3, Proposition
3.17, Proposition 3.18] and the fact that in the proof of the fact that WTP and WHIP imply
uniform rectifiability in [4, Chapter II, §3.5] just requires small enough paramters, finally prove
that µ is uniformly rectifiable.
